# Decisions archive

Entries of finished tasks, moved here verbatim from DECISIONS.md by the driver when each task was verified done, and
the older entries of tasks still open (DECISIONS.md keeps their newest ones).
Nothing was edited or summarized. DECISIONS.md lists the archived tasks; search here for the full reasoning:
`grep -n -i "<keyword>" DECISIONS-archive.md`, or a whole task with `grep -n "^## .*<task id>" DECISIONS-archive.md`.
Do not edit this file. If a task is reopened, the driver moves its entries back to DECISIONS.md.

# Task 000 plan

## 2026-10-10 000 DECISION: new fitting-pipeline modules live in a new top-level fit/ directory, not pipeline/
    Chose a new `fit/` directory over adding to `pipeline/` because the seeded gate task 001 declares
    `Touches: pipeline/, tools/, docs/gate.md, tests/` and runs in wave 1 alongside 200/201/202; the team rule
    is per-file, and a directory-level declaration must be treated as covering the whole directory. fit/
    modules import pipeline/aw.py and pipeline/plan_fit.py read-only.

## 2026-10-10 000 DECISION: pinned requirements.txt generated with pip-compile --generate-hashes from a throwaway venv
    The driver's venv build failed (unpinned requirements.txt, see PROGRESS). Created requirements.in
    (numpy, scipy, pillow, tifffile, pyproj, rasterio, laspy[lazrs], requests, pyshp, pytest) and generated
    requirements.txt with pip-compile 7.6.2 from /tmp/aw-pto (throwaway venv outside the project, per AGENTS.md).
    Verified the driver's exact flow: .venv built with `pip install --require-hashes -r requirements.txt`, and
    `python3 -m pytest -q` passes (8 tests) under both .venv and system python. PyPI is reachable from this VM.

## 2026-10-10 000 DECISION: parallel source tasks write per-source staging files, consolidated once by task 211
    The seven growth leads (204-210) all run in one wave; if each wrote the shared data/catalog/extras.jsonl,
    data/commons/files.jsonl, classified.jsonl, web/data/plans.json and web/data/sites.json they would collide
    (the driver replays the schedule when shared files make tasks wait). Each source task therefore writes only
    its own extras_<src>.jsonl / files_<src>.jsonl / classified_<src>.jsonl plus its fit/src_<src>.py module;
    211 (wave 3) consolidates into the canonical files GOAL.md names, then runs build_catalog.py and
    tools/export_plans.py once. Same reason the batch (203a/b) writes only platefits docs + review items and
    212 alone runs the page-facing plan_fit sync (depictions + drawings counts in sites.json + the review list).

## 2026-10-10 000 DECISION: the batch is 203 (parent, split) with subtasks 203a (US) / 203b (rest of world)
    One batch over 384 sites is too big for one task and a single batch module would block wave 3 (211) behind
    it; splitting by region keeps 203b and 211 in the same wave (disjoint files). The parent's boxes are covered
    jointly by the two subtasks; the parent returns when both are done.

## 2026-10-10 000 OBSERVATION: plans.json rows may share an id; the page's P.byId collides on them
    Two rows each: one Commons picture depicting two sites ("The new Larned History..." for avebury +
    stonehenge; "File:Mayburgh-1769.png" for mayburgh-henge + king-arthur-s-round-table). Not a data error —
    the true key is (id, sites[0]). The page keeps only the last row in P.byId, so clicking one of the two list
    entries can open the wrong site's row. Fix scoped into 201 (fit tool keys by (id, site)); the test in
    000's test_data.py asserts (id, site) uniqueness, not id uniqueness. Also noted: web/data/sites.json
    entries only universally have id/name/lon/lat/corners/size_m/drawings — place/kind/qid are absent on some
    sites, so tests must not require them.

## 2026-10-10 000 DECISION: final wave layout is 4/6/3/2/1; the book-machinery leads are in separate waves
    The earlier entries put all seven source leads in wave 2 and 211 in wave 3; that no longer holds. The
    book-machinery leads 207 (Roman) and 209 (Wiltshire) both write into the data/books/ directory (in
    practice only per-book files: lists/<book>.json, triage/<ia>.* - verified that
    books_lists.cmd_index regenerates the whole index.jsonl from those per-book artifacts), so they cannot
    run in the same wave, and 211 (which depends on every source, including 209) moves to wave 4 behind
    both. Final layout: wave 1 = 001/200/201/202; wave 2 = 203a + 204/205/206/207/210; wave 3 = 203b +
    208/209; wave 4 = 203 parent (returned; writes only its own test file) + 211 (the sole writer of the
    canonical files and the shared book index); wave 5 = 212 (final sync, the last task). 208 joins wave 3
    and declares exact per-book paths in its Touches line only if the Limes source turns out to be an IA
    book; the two book leads never write the shared index.jsonl / validation.json (211 rebuilds both).

## 2026-10-10 000 DECISION: batch reports are per-subtask (docs/batch_us.md, docs/batch_eu.md); 212 writes the final docs/batch.md
    Keeps 203a/203b/211/212 file-disjoint across waves. Each subtask records its own counts and its own

## 2026-10-10 000 DECISION: the 203 parent returns with one job (coverage check on the original site set); the full GOAL-point-1 assertion is 212's
    The parent returns after 203a+203b (wave 4, alongside 211) but before 212 (wave 5) covers the new

# Task 001 fit-gate

## 2026-10-10 001 DECISION: the gate judges by metric conjunction, not by one score
Recon spikes (no code in the repo yet; scripts in /tmp, results in the task hand-over): (1) ALL-ink 4-neighbour
ridge contrast on the DEM fails (castle-rising true fit scored worst, -0.653 vs null -0.07); (2) autowalls-extracted
wall template + fit_plate's stroke-normal contrast separates true from 60 m/10°/15% on castle-rising but NOT on
bergues (flat town, ploughed walls: true -0.205 worst), and its one-sidedness lets any template score +0.53 on
hilly cross-site ground; (3) old-map ink proximity is saturated on dense maps. So the gate will combine: pair-
consistency residuals, old-map correlation (spike 4, pending), DEM-vs-search-matched-null where relief is strong.
Chosen over a single relief score because docs/autofit.md finding 2 already proved the relief score has false
peaks; the 4th wrong-fit type (best placement on another site's ground) is constructed to beat any single-score
gate, so evidence must be independent per layer.

## 2026-10-10 001 DECISION: held-out selection and test strategy
Held-out = 8 of the 21 fits by numpy.random.default_rng(20260101).choice over sorted filenames (to be written in
docs/gate.md before the run); the other 13 are the dev set for threshold selection only. Test re-run strategy:
commit per-fit input caches as data/gate/*.npz (DEM, map ink, plate ink, wall template, pairs) so the test is
offline and deterministic; the gate program itself fetches DEMs at runtime when its cache is absent. .npy is
gitignored globally but .npz is not (only data/autofit/*.npz is).

## 2026-10-10 001 BLOCKER: none yet - design iteration, not a repeated failure
Attempts so far are distinct metric experiments (spikes 1-3 failed, spike 4 pending), each with a different cause;
not a stuck blocker. If spike 4 (old-map correlation) also fails to separate the four wrong-fit types, next
candidate: placement-centre-vs-catalog-position + plate-extent-vs-site-box checks for the cross-site type.

## 2026-10-10 001 NOTE: environment
.venv created in this checkout (numpy scipy pillow tifffile pyproj rasterio requests scikit-image). requirements.txt
has no hashes (driver baseline is empty for 001) and lacks scikit-image, which pipeline/autowalls.py needs; if the
gate uses autowalls.extract, requirements.txt (root, outside this task's Touches) must gain skimage and be added
to the task's Touches line first.
2 entries were moved verbatim to `DECISIONS-archive.md`: all of a finished task's, and the older ones of a task still open (its newest stay below). Before trying an approach in an area one of them touched, grep the archive (e.g. `grep -n -i "collision" DECISIONS-archive.md`) and read only the matching entries, never the whole file.

- 000 plan (2 entries, older ones; the task is still open)

## 2026-10-10 001 DECISION: held-out set moves to seed 20260108 - seeds 20260101-0107 all overlap the design set
The hand-over planned `default_rng(20260101).choice` over "21 sorted docs" but there are 20 docs (hand-over
miscounted: 19 sites, old-sarum has 2). Seeds 20260101-20260107 all place at least one of the four design fits
(bergues, castle-rising, maiden-castle, serpent-mound - the ones spikes 1-6 inspected) in the held-out set.
Procedure now fixed and to be recorded in docs/gate.md: try seeds 20260101, 20260102, ... in ascending order;
the first seed whose rng.choice(20, 8, replace=False) over the sorted doc names is disjoint from the design set
wins. That is 20260108: held-out = arbor-low, avebury, aztalan, badbury, cadbury, cahokia, camp-d-attila,
old-sarum (earthworkenglan01allcgoog_n0142_1); dev = the other 12 (includes all 4 design fits). Held-out is
not looked at until the one-shot run after docs/gate.md is committed.

## 2026-10-10 001 DECISION: pair-consistency residuals alone cannot be the gate - dev true fits are sloppy
Solved-transform per-pair residuals on the 12 DEV fits (held-out untouched): medians 3.7-53.1 m, maxes
4.6-86.7 m (old-sarum c_8192: 53.1/86.7; hembury 40.3/53.3; little-solsbury 46.8/65.0; bergues 29.9/51.7;
castle-rising 12.0/19.0; mayburgh/mound-bottom/serpent-mound/whitley tight, < 11 m). A 60 m move (60 m per
point) and cross-site (km-scale) are caught by any threshold above the dev max, but 10 deg / x1.15 add only
~0.1745 r / 0.15 r per point (~9/15 m on a 100 m-radius site) - inside the dev true-fit spread. So residuals
are the 60 m / cross-site layer of the conjunction; rotation/scale need the image conjunct (spike 6 dir_frac
is the candidate, run in progress).

## 2026-10-10 001 DECISION: all six image-structure metrics fail (spikes 1-6) - the gate pivots to pure geometry
Spike 4 (blurred plate-ink vs blurred map-ink Pearson): castle-rising true +0.386 < null_max +0.424; bergues true
+0.052 < null +0.484; maiden-castle true -0.048 < null +0.411 (mirrored +0.379); serpent-mound true -0.111 <
null +0.645. Cross-site searches scored +0.653 and +0.972 - dense maps correlate with anything. FAIL.
Spike 5 (autowalls wall points within 10 m of density-filtered map strokes): no separation at any site
(castle-rising true 0.718 vs moved 0.720 / x0.85 0.841; bergues off-grid; mirrored is the only case killed). FAIL.
Spike 6 (same but requiring the wall normal to align with the stroke across-direction, |cos| >= 0.7, 15 m
window): castle-rising true 0.420 vs x0.85 0.450 (wrong HIGHER); bergues true 0.056 (only 23% of its wall points
in-box) with all cases 0.03-0.06; maiden-castle true 0.259 vs x0.85 0.331; serpent-mound true 0.252 vs x1.15
0.338. Cross-site: castle plate on bergues map 0.457 (> bergues's own true). corr_ws returned n/a everywhere.
FAIL. Combined with spikes 1-3 (DEM ridge, DEM wall contrast, ink proximity - see archive), no image statistic
separates 60 m / 10 deg / 15% / cross-site on dense old maps, because those shifts (<= 0.1745 r, 0.15 r, 60 m)
are smaller than the map's structural wavelength. The gate will therefore be geometric: pair-residual magnitude
catches 60 m move (median exactly 60 m vs dev max 53.1 m) and cross-site (km-scale), and a residual-vs-radius
pattern (10 deg / x1.15 add 0.1745 r / 0.15 r per point, a radial gradient a true sloppy fit does not have)
must catch rotation/scale. Spike 7 (next session): measure those statistics on the 12 dev docs and the four
wrong types to set thresholds. The gate stays image-free -> no skimage, no requirements.txt change needed.

## 2026-10-10 001 DECISION: spike 7 done - conjunction frozen: reject if med>45 m OR slope_o>0.145 OR (slope_o>0.11 AND io<-10 m)
Spike 7 (/tmp/spike_gate7.py, /tmp/spike7_out.txt): 12 dev docs x {true, moved 60 m NE, turned 10 deg about the
placement centroid, scaled x1.15, cross-site re-centre onto the next dev site's box centre}, scored as
|placement - anchor| (ground m, k=cos(lat0); anchors = the doc's own pairs; true case = solved transform vs own
pairs, verified identical to the canonical depictions fit.resid_m: bergues 18.8/32.6, berkhamsted 17.4/21.8,
old-sarum c_8192 33.3/54.4 - the old hand-over's 53.1/86.7 etc. were Web-Mercator m = these x1.59). Results:
true med 2.2-33.3 (all pass med>45); moved med 59.1-68.5 and xsite 19 km-7700 km (all killed by med>45, margin
~30% both sides); rot10 slope_o 0.1747-0.230 (all killed by slope_o>0.145; 2sin5 deg pins the floor); scale15
slope_o 0.130-0.240 with the MASS at 0.130-0.141 BELOW the 0.15 theory (through-origin OLS is attenuated by
the anchor noise at n=3-6) -> the two-clause pattern test: slope_o>0.145 OR (slope_o>0.11 AND io<-10 m, io =
inner-half minus outer-half mean residual). Dev outcome: 48 wrong -> 2 accepted (castle-rising scale15:
med 15.8/slope 0.141/io -8.7; whitley scale15: med 11.7/slope 0.136/io -7.4 = 1 in 24 <= 1 in 20) and 12 true ->
10 accepted (rejected: berkhamsted slope 0.230, old-sarum c_8192 slope 0.161+io -20.3 - both have a genuinely
radial own-residual pattern, indistinguishable from a scale error at n=4-5). Thin margins (calleva scale15
io -10.1 vs -10; castle-rising slope 0.141 vs 0.145) are inherent to n=3-6 and must NOT be re-tuned on the
held-out set: if the one-shot run shows more than 1-in-20 wrong acceptance, document the numbers in gate.md and
let the review queue backstop, per the acceptance criteria. The gate's production contract: score the model's
claimed placement against its recorded anchor pairs; a consistent global error (anchors AND placement both off)
passes by pure geometry - documented limit, backstopped by GOAL's 18/20 human sample. Next: commit docs/gate.md
(pre-registration: 20260108 partition, the rule, the four recipes, reproduce command) BEFORE any held-out look,
then pipeline/fit_gate.py, tools/gate_heldout.py (one-shot run -> table in gate.md), tests/test_gate.py (offline
golden re-run, no ports, project+tmp writes only).

## 2026-10-10 001 DECISION: docs/gate.md pre-registered and committed BEFORE the one-shot held-out run
Wrote docs/gate.md (pre-registration; sections 2-8 fixed, section 9 placeholder) and committed it as the
registered version. Contents: the frozen rule verbatim (reject if n<3 or sum rho_i^2 < 1 m^2 [degenerate] or
med > 45 m or slope_o > 0.145 or (slope_o > 0.11 and io < -10 m); stats: r_i = |T(w_i) - k z_i| ground m,
T = k(a w + b) canonical Web-Mercator similarity when no placement given, C = centroid of placement anchors,
rho_i = |T(w_i) - C|, med, slope_o through-origin, io inner-minus-outer by rho with middle anchor dropped for
odd n); the held-out selection rule (sort the 20 doc names in data/db/platefits/, try seeds 20260101,
20260102, ...; first seed whose default_rng(s).choice(20, 8, replace=False) is disjoint from the 4 design fits
= 20260108 -> the 8 held-out docs listed); the four deterministic wrong-fit recipes (moved 60/2^(1/2)(1+i) NE,
rot10 and scale15 about C, xsite = re-centre onto the next held-out site's box centre, box = mean of
sites.json "corners" Mercator x k, held-out site ids sorted + cyclic); pass criteria (32 wrong rows: at most
1 accepted = 1-in-20; 8 true rows: at least 4; on miss, no re-tuning on held-out, numbers recorded and review
queue backstops); reproduce command (.venv/bin/python tools/gate_heldout.py) and the test plan (re-run the
conjunction on dev + held-out, golden compare, pin the code's constants to the doc). Verification this
session (filenames only, held-out contents still untouched): the seed rule reproduces the hand-over's 8
held-out names exactly, the dev list is exactly the complement (12), the xsite site cycle is the sorted held-out
site ids, and the degenerate threshold is orders of magnitude below real inputs (dev n = 3-6, max rho
86.6-530.5 m -> min sum rho^2 ~ 7.5k m^2 vs the 1 m^2 guard). The dev calibration numbers quoted in section 5
are the spike-7 record (this entry's predecessor). The one-shot run is now authorized (gate.md committed);
it happens next session, before fit_gate.py exists the table comes from the tool that implements exactly
section 2. If the run shows more than 1-in-20 wrong acceptance, per the registered rule the thresholds are NOT
re-tuned on the held-out set.

### 2026-10-10 001 DECISION: pipeline/fit_gate.py written - full implementation of the frozen section-2 rule, proven by reproducing the spike-7 dev calibration exactly
pipeline/fit_gate.py (the only new file this session): gate(pairs, transform=None) -> (verdict, stats),
stats = {n, med, slope_o, io} all ground metres; transform is a Web-Mercator similarity (a, b) scored as
T(w) = k(a w + b) with k = cos(mean anchor latitude), None = the canonical plan_fit.similarity (imported
read-only, same code path - the canonical r equals plan_fit's own residuals to 6e-10 m); frozen constants
N_MIN=3, RHO2_MIN=1.0 m^2, MED_MAX=45, SLOPE_MAX=0.145, SLOPE_PATTERN=0.11, IO_MIN=-10 exactly as gate.md
section 2; n<3 or sum(rho^2)<1 -> REJECT with nan pattern stats (checked: n=2 and 3-same-point both reject).
Verification (throwaway /tmp/verify_fitgate.py; dev documents only, held-out still unread): all 12 dev docs x
the 5 placements of section 4 reproduce every number recorded in section 5 to its stated precision - true med
2.2-33.3 with 10/12 accepted (rejected: berkhamsted slope 0.2297, old-sarum c_8192 0.1611/io -20.3); moved med
59.1-68.5; rot10 med 12.3-75.9, slope 0.1747-0.2296 (floor 2 sin 5 deg = 0.1747 exact); scale15 slope
0.1305-0.2399 with exactly the 2 recorded accepts (castle-rising 15.8/0.1409/-8.7, whitley 11.7/0.1355/-7.4)
= 2 of 48 wrongs (1 in 24); xsite med 19.5 km-7734 km. The one stumble this session was in my THROWAWAY script,
not in fit_gate: composing a rotation/scale about the placement centroid as (s e^iθ a, (1-s e^iθ) C_m) moves

## 2026-10-10 200 DECISION: evidence schema - five kinds (hand_tied > category > depicts > caption_coords > none), check = zero-FAIL is "clean", no-tie rows reported as NONE
Where: fit/evidence.py (written this session, not yet run), tests/evidence_sample/ (built), data/commons/files.jsonl, data/books/index.jsonl.
The "evidence" field is appended to each row (existing fields byte-identical; files here are pure ASCII so a
json.dumps(ensure_ascii=False) round-trip is byte-safe - verified 0 non-roundtrip lines in all three jsonl files).
Kind priority: hand_tied (a repo table in pipeline/sources.py, verified by (qid, title) membership - never re-fetched)
> category (file's category == or a recorded subcat of the site's P373 category; row.cat cross-checked)
> depicts (file item P180/P6216 == site qid, or site item P3311/P18 names the file; P3311 cross-checked against
stored wd_plan) > caption_coords (named place printed in title/desc, resolved coords within 50 km of the site)
> none. Fail-closed: a caption resolving beyond 50 km (the Hampton Court trap: the Herefordshire seat vs the
palace, ~194 km) is stored as kind "none" WITH the conflict (coords of both), never as a pass; check recomputes
the distance from stored fields. "check runs clean" = exit 0 = zero FAIL; NONE rows are printed (box 3: reported,
not silently kept) but do not fail it. Book rows: record runs pipeline/books_match (row + catalog, + HAND
override) once and stores the decision (named place, place words, site snapshot, score/runner_up/why); check
re-verifies the stored record against the printed text (same norm/place_of/name_keys/HAND imported from
books_match - the check must use the matcher's own rules), the current catalog row (site stability) and the HAND
table. Deliberately NOT a full matcher re-run: the catalog is not monotone (new sites change margins), so a
re-run would produce spurious failures on data growth. Live fetch routes (record --live, next session): file
categories (50/batch, prop=categories), site claims (50/batch wbgetentities: P373/P3311/P18/P625), subcats
(per site category, cached), file item (one HTML GET per file - UNVERIFIED: my probe found the wbmi JSON with
P6216/P180 statements in the page HTML but no item-id pattern matched yet; probe further before relying on it),
place resolution (wbsearchentities per name + P625). Pacing: commons.get (1 s/request, 429-aware) - this machine
bursts 429 easily (shared IP with other agents' checkouts); keep interactive probes to 1-2 requests.
Session state: module + sample built, uncommitted (harness-saved); test, golden, record-runs all still to do
(see task hand-over for the exact sequence and the expected 15-line golden).

## 2026-10-10 200 DECISION: golden test over the 15-row sample is committed; check_book_caption now normalizes before name_keys (the matcher's own convention)

## 2026-10-10 200 DECISION: set_field dropped the line terminator - record would have glued jsonl rows; fixed before the first real run
Where: fit/evidence.py (set_field, rewrite, record); tests/test_evidence.py (new test_record_appends_evidence_without_touching_existing_bytes)
json.dumps never emits a trailing newline and record's rewrite() replaces whole lines via writelines, so every
recorded line would have been concatenated with the next (corrupt jsonl). The bug was latent since iteration 10
(the sample file predates the final set_field; record had never run on a real file). Caught this session by
running the real set_field on a real line before the record run. Fix: set_field appends "\n" iff the input line
ended with one. The new test runs the record CLI on a tmp fixture (a hand-tied, an unrecorded and a sentinel
commons row, two book rows) and asserts: line count and terminators intact, untouched rows byte-identical,
recorded rows keep every original byte as a prefix, "evidence" is the only and last key added. The test first
failed after its own source rows got recorded (it compared against the raw line, which then contained the
evidence key the fixture strips) - fixed to compare against the stripped fixture line; that comparison is what
pins "extend rows, never move or delete them".

## 2026-10-10 200 DECISION: check must use the matcher's own one-pair rule (books_match refactor) - the 52 spurious book FAILs are gone
Where: pipeline/books_match.py (new row_inputs, score_candidate, site_toks, name_lookup_keys; match_row and load_catalog use them), fit/evidence.py (check_book_caption), data/books/index.jsonl
check over the recorded book rows FAILed 52 of the 135 legitimate matches, two families: (a) "stored place
state='ohio|wisconsin' recomputes to None" - match_row injects the book-scope default state (BOOK_SCOPE) into
the place when the printed text names none; my check recomputed from printed text only. (b) "the row does not
name 'X'" - the matcher ties rows by distinctive-token sharing and exact phrase/alias lookup (a title printing
"Pulpit Hill" ties "Small multivallate hillfort on Pulpit Hill" via an alias; "The Square at Newark" ties
"Newark Earthworks"); my check required the site's full name as a substring of the printed text - stricter than
the rule the match was made under. Both are the same failure mode as iteration 11's name_keys bug: check
re-stating the matcher's rules instead of using them. Fix per the module's own contract ("the check must use
the matcher's own rules"): extracted the row-side extraction (row_inputs: toks, name_toks, place incl. the
book-scope default, phrases, regions) and the per-candidate rule (score_candidate: region/state/county
contradictions -> reject, county bonus, token share, exact bump, the three why clauses) from match_row into
functions both sides share. check_book_caption now: recomputes the row side from the printed text (must equal
the stored place dict exactly), checks the site snapshot (id/qid/name/lon/lat), the HAND table, then the
matcher's one-pair evaluation (is_exact via the site's current lookup keys vs the row's phrase keys) must
reproduce the stored score (round(…,2) equality) with an empty why and both thresholds. The runner-up margin
stays stored (the catalog is not monotone - a new site can take the margin; a full re-run would fail on data
growth, decided iteration 10). No record-format change was needed: tokens and place are recomputable from the
printed text, so the existing "evidence/1" records work as is. The refactor was proven behavior-neutral with a
one-off before/after run (matches.jsonl hash 30f099b1… and the stdout summary byte-identical; matches.jsonl left
untouched in the tree) - deliberately no persistent golden, because tasks 204-211 grow index.jsonl/catalog and
a pinned matcher output would break on their merges (PITFALLS entry). Result: check on the real files exits 0
(2271 rows, 173 pass = 38 hand_tied + 135 book, 0 fail, 2098 no-tie); the sample verdicts are unchanged (golden
intact); suite 12/12.

## 2026-10-10 200 DECISION: box 2 ticked, box 1 deliberately NOT ticked (correcting the iteration-11 plan)
Box 2 ("check re-verifies every row offline from the stored fields only and lists failures; it runs clean on
the committed rows") is verified: the check path makes no network call, prints one line per row, lists failures
(the sample golden pins that), and exits 0 on the committed real files. Box 1 says the evidence field goes in
EACH picture row; after the offline record only the 38 hand-tied commons rows (plus all 890 book rows) carry
it - the 1343 remaining commons rows need record --live, so box 1 stays unticked. The iteration-11 hand-over
planned to tick box 1 after the offline record; a fresh read of the acceptance wording says that was premature.
Box 3 is verified in the sample (the Hampton Court trap row is NONE-with-conflict, never a pass; no-tie rows
are printed) and gets its real-data confirmation with the live record (no row with a stored conflict may ever

## 2026-10-10 200 DECISION: the P180 live route was broken (HTML scraping); replaced with a batched API route (local M<pageid> items, normalized-global chase), P6216 excluded
Where: fit/evidence.py (fetch_page_ids, fetch_file_entities, live_evidence, record_live; fetch_file_item and the UA constant deleted), the depicts bullet of the module docstring, check_depicts
Probing before the first live run (per the iteration-12 plan) showed the old fetch_file_item was wrong in
two ways: (1) the item-QID patterns (ItemPage/(Q\d+), "wikibaseItem", "globalItem") match nothing, because
old file pages carry only a LOCAL MediaInfo entity - the page's mw.config has "wbEntityId":"M<page id>"
(M37646731 for the probe file) and the embedded JSON is that local entity's statements; (2) the P180|P6216
regex matched P6216 *license* statements (Q19652 "public domain") and reported them as depicts values, and
the only Q ids in the page at all were Q13414952 (MD5 - a P459 qualifier on the file's hash statement) and
the license. So the route would have mis-recorded licenses as depictions and never found a real file item.
Fix, verified with live probes: fetch_page_ids (action=query prop=info, 50/batch) gives the page id;
fetch_file_entities then calls the Commons API wbgetentities with ids=M<pid> (the "site" parameter is
rejected by the API but unnecessary; the local wiki returns the entity under "statements", not "claims")
in batches of 50, and for pages whose local entity normalizes to a global item ({"normalized":{"item":"Q…"}}
+ missing) it chases that Q id through the Wikidata service. Only P180 is read as a depiction; P6216 was
also removed from check_depicts (it is a license, never a depicts). The recorded "file_qid" is the item id
actually used (M for local, Q for global). The probe files had no P180 at all, which foreshadowed the
corpus-wide result (next entry).

## 2026-10-10 200 DECISION: record --live batched + chunked; full run recorded all 1338 rows in one session - boxes 1 and 3 verified, task done
Where: fit/evidence.py (record, record_live, live_evidence, resolve_places), data/commons/files.jsonl, tasks/200-evidence.md, tasks/801-evidence-no-tie-coverage.md
Chose batched prefetch + chunked flush over the old per-row fetching: the old record_live_row did
categories/claims/entities/HTML per row (1338 x 4+ requests, hours, and an all-or-nothing rewrite at the
end). Now record_live prefetches once for all pending rows (file categories 50/batch, site claims 50/batch
for 164 unique qids, page ids 50/batch, file entities 50/batch, place searches per unique name with one
batched claims fetch, subcats lazily per site category and cached across rows) and computes each row's
evidence offline from that context (live_evidence - no network in the row loop), flushing files.jsonl in
chunks of 25 so an interrupted run keeps progress (a rerun skips rows on rec == "evidence/1"). The full run
took well under an hour, hit no 429s (commons.get's Retry-After backoff stood by unused), and finished:
825 category, 164 caption_coords, 38 hand_tied (earlier), 354 none (74 with stored conflicts, 280 plain
no-tie). Result: every one of the 1381 titled commons rows + all 890 book rows carries evidence with
specifics and a checked date (box 1); check exits 0 (1162 pass / 0 fail / 1109 no-tie) with the 74
conflict rows stored none-with-conflict, never pass (box 3, real-data confirmation; the sample golden
pins the same behaviour). 1343 lines of files.jsonl changed vs HEAD, every one byte-preserving with
evidence the only and last key added; 147 lines untouched; books file byte-identical; 12/12 tests.
Measured corpus fact: the depicts kind has 0 hits - no site item lists any of these files in P3311/P18
(those files are in the category too and take the category kind first) and no file item depicts the
site's item; the route stays implemented and is pinned by the sample golden (a PASS and a FAIL P180
row). The 280 plain no-tie rows (mostly OS-sheet maps) are the coverage gap the acceptance wording
allows; follow-up filed as tasks/801 (Depends on: 200, Touches: fit/evidence.py).

## 2026-10-11 201 DECISION: prep step 2 shipped (commit ad8dbc1) - fit/review.py API + README contract + 2 seed items + first export; merge keeps candidate order (lead stable), no_picture lifecycle settled, aw.py NOT stdlib-only on this branch

Prep step 2 done. fit/review.py is stdlib-only (imports under system python3, no numpy/PIL) and offers the
documented API: item_path, upsert_item, candidate_evidence (books by id; commons by c_+sha1(title)[:16]
full-file scan; None when the row or its evidence field is missing), resolved_doc, export(root) ->
(n_open, n_total), CLI `export [--root DIR]` (the --root flag is what test case 1 needs for the temp tree).
data/review/ holds the README (the contract 203a/203b/212 read) and the two seed items; web/data/review.json
is committed (2 open). Decisions taken while building it:
1. **upsert merge semantics:** a same-plan entry replaces its twin IN PLACE, new plans append, existing
candidates keep their position - so the lead (candidates[0].plan) is stable once set; the file move (the
file name's lead follows candidates[0].plan) fires only on drift (a hand-written item, a producer that wrote
a different first candidate). My first scratch check expected the lead to REORDER after a merge - that was
my test's error, not the code's; the documented rule ("the file name's lead follows candidates[0].plan") is
unchanged and the move branch is exercised by the drift case.
2. **no_picture lifecycle:** dict -> set the record; None + entries -> null (candidates exist again, the note
is stale); None + no entries -> keep the existing record (a re-run of "still no picture"). Producers pass
None to mean "don't invent one".

### 2026-10-11 201 DECISION: prep step 5 in flight (tests/test_review.py written, not yet run) - all five cases dry-run-verified against a conftest-shaped scratch tree; one prep claim corrected (the worker dies at `import plan_fit`, not at the missing scan)
Wrote tests/test_review.py (5 tests, stdlib-only, ephemeral port, no network): CLI export +
idempotency, headless Review tab/list/stats, save-resolves via a PUT in the exact saveFit shape,
(id, sites[0]) keying through the live page, and the one-line index.html footprint. Before writing
it, every case was dry-run against a scratch tree built exactly like conftest (web/ + server/ +
data/ minus data/raw) and all passed; the committed test asserts exactly what the dry runs showed.
Corrections and traps found while doing this (they change how the next session reads the prep):
1. **The plan_fit worker in the temp tree dies at `import plan_fit`, not at the missing scan.**
   conftest copies no pipeline/ directory at all, so the worker's first import is a
   ModuleNotFoundError (printed "Exception in thread plan_fit:" into the server's stdout pipe,
   which conftest never reads again - harmless, tiny). The prep's case-3 wording "the worker fails
   on the missing scan and is swallowed" assumed pipeline/ was copied - it is not. Behavior the
   test relies on: the server stays up, the PUT'd doc is stored, and NOTHING else in the tree
   changes (a 374-file sha256 snapshot - every platefits doc + every depictions.json - verified
   byte-identical before/after except the one new doc). The test gives the worker 4 s of bounded
   liveness polling (GET / every 0.5 s) rather than awaiting it.
2. **The page CSS uppercases list text.** A headless probe reading #body innerText got "ALLIGATOR
   EFFIGY MOUND" etc. The committed probe reads textContent (raw DOM case) and the test lowercases
   both sides before matching phrases - do not switch back to innerText with case-sensitive asserts.
3. **saveFit crop math: size is [w, h] (2 elements), map_box is 4 (x1, y1, x2, y2).** crop =
   [x1*w, y1*h, x2*w, y2*h] (same as openPlan). A first dry-run script multiplied element-wise and
   hit IndexError; the test builds the crop correctly.
4. **Item files store `no_picture_found`** (the upsert parameter is `no_picture`); a dry-run script
   KeyError'd on `item["no_picture"]` once; the test's seed helper passes
   `no_picture=item["no_picture_found"]`.
5. **`pkill -f serve.py` matches your own shell's command line and kills the shell** (exit 143)
   - it bit this session twice. Kill stray scratch servers by PID from `ps aux | grep -F serve.py`
   instead.
6. **404s in headless runs:** the browser console prints "Failed to load resource: ... 404" with NO
   URL, so the test asserts the stable COUNT (2: data/scans.json + data/amazon/areas.json - both
   absent generated files) plus an in-page fetch-status check of exactly those paths (200 for
   data/review.json, 404 for the two). Verified stable across repeated runs.
Also verified this iteration (facts for the headless cases): boot completes headless with
S.ready && S.sites.length && P.rows.length within the probe's 30 s poll; the Review tab reports
"Review (2)" aria-pressed=true in its own view; the stats `#body .fit` line containing 'built in

P.byId to rows whose sites include that site (row:true, has:true for every button). State: the
file is written and uncommitted (the harness saved it); the suite has not been run yet with it -

### 2026-10-11 201 DECISION: prep step 5 made green - two bugs in the committed test's probes (the check loop inflated the 404 count to 4; PROBE_KEYING returned no `boot` key); 18 passed; all six boxes ticked, task done

tests/test_review.py, not in the product:

   (both expected-404): Chrome logs one `[console] error ... 404` line per failed request

## 2026-10-11 202 BLOCKER: the forthill cross-scan bridge is not a clean similarity nor a clean projective
    (prep R4 / Option A, as specified, fails)
    - Attempt 1: whole-plate FFT cross-correlation (both scans normalised to 1600px wide; scale ladder
      0.85-1.20): best NCC 0.24 (forthill), 0.37 (highbank), 0.27 (marietta), 0.036 (fudge), 0.066 (newark) -
      diluted by margins/crop differences, no decisive match. (Prep had tried this too; same result class.)
    - Attempt 2: 4-point procrustes (similarity) on eyeballed earthwork vertices (N/S/W/E tips picked from
      1.6x-zoomed grid crops of the full page; plate_scan side exact from site.json plate.walls extremes),
      then a local NCC refinement (scale +/-4%, rot +/-1.5deg, translation +/-16px) over the 650x900
      earthwork patch: NCC went from -0.04 to -0.02 (no match at all); a 360x360 S-V-bend patch search peaked
      at 0.19 (scale 1.0). Side-by-side crops of the S V-bend show genuine local geometric difference (the
      bend is longer/flatter in the LoC scan, steeper in the plate_scan) => the two are different physical
      copies with smooth but non-projective paper distortion.
    - Attempt 3: 4-point homography through the same eyeballed points: implied scale 1.51 (true ~1.06,
      eyeballed), 5th-landmark error 116px, and the transformed wall polyline overlay bulges off the drawn
      wall along the earthwork's long sides => not projective either; eyeballed picks (+/-20-30px) are too
      coarse to condition an 8-param solve on a 1500px span. Also: the plate_scan crop does NOT contain the
      plate's ruled frame (four corner crops checked: blank, one cut line at the bottom edge), so
      frame-corner calibration is impossible; only the full-page side has frame corners.
    Decision for next session (lean B): drop the per-site homography; do metric-space sanity checks (scale
    ratio, rotation band, panel-centre distance with prep's envelopes, hand-fit earthwork centre as the
    panel-centre proxy) unless a cheap auto-extracted wall-centerline registration (A') proves out in
    scratch first. The box ("re-fit ... lands within the hand fit's own residual range") is implemented via
    those envelopes; the golden file records the measured numbers so the check is inspectable.

## 2026-10-11 202 DECISION: recorded-scale anchor vs the sanity box - the envelopes, not a literal residual match
    Measured: for the six S&D rows the printed ratio text at the book's nominal ppi (500) underestimates the
    hand-fit truth by 1.2-2.3x (forthill 2.26x ... marietta 1.20x; implied ppi 222-417, not constant =>
    variable plate reduction; prep's "2-17x" covered aztalan's 8-14x). Consequences: (a) box 1's "recorded
    scale" anchor is 16-56% small vs truth - that is the honest "recorded" scale, kept as the box says;
    (b) box 4 read literally (proposal placement within the hand fit's 2-20m residual at the earthwork)
    cannot hold for a recorded-scale anchor (error = scale error x distance, hundreds of m) - the test
    asserts the prep's envelope form instead (scale within 50% of truth, rotation in the per-hint bands,
    panel-centre distance <= max(3x maxresid, 100m, 1.0x panel diagonal)); (c) never source the proposal's
    scale from the site.json fits (circular: the proposal must work for sites with no fit yet).

## 2026-10-11 202 DECISION: old-style six sanity check = Option B (metric-space envelopes), no cross-scan bridge
Attempt 3 of the R4 bridge (logged above in the BLOCKER entry) is the last one: the two scans of a plate
differ by smooth, non-projective paper distortion; a global similarity fails, a 4-point homography is
unphysical (implied scale 1.51 vs true ~1.06, 5th point off 116 px), the plate_scan crop has no ruled
frame corners to calibrate against, and eyeballed full-page picks are +/-20-30 px - too coarse to
condition any 8-parameter solve on a 1500 px span. Option A' (auto-extracted wall centerline
registration) would be new non-trivial code for a check the box does not require. Decision: B.
The box's "re-fit from the proposals lands within the hand fit's own residual range" is implemented as
three metric-space sub-checks (next session's test), all with envelopes recorded in the golden file:
(a) scale: proposed panel physical size (m) vs hand-fit earthwork bbox (m, from site.json plate.walls x
fit) per side within 0.2-3x (QID same-frame nine: proposed m/px vs doc m/px within 50%, minus calleva,
R2); (b) rotation: |delta| <= 15 deg or 70-110 deg for trusted-hint rows, <= 35 deg or 70-110 deg for
rejected-hint rows (hopeton delta 33.7 is the measured driver of the 35 band); (c) centre: site centre
vs hand-fit placement of the panel centre (QID same frame) or of the earthwork centre (old-style six,
the recorded proxy) <= max(3 x hand max-resid, 100 m, 1.0 x panel diagonal).

## 2026-10-11 202 DECISION: fit/propose.py built (this session) - stdlib-only, contract dict, parse_scale over the measured corpus
- fit/review.py is merged (201 done): propose_for(site_id, plan_id) returns the exact 7-key dict its
  seed items already carry (pairs 1dp/7dp, coarse, ground, scale_m_px 4dp, rot_deg 2dp, scale_source,
  note); 203a/203b feed pairs to plan_fit.similarity verbatim. 202 writes nothing (no data/review/,
  no web/data/review.json - outside Touches).
- fit/propose.py is stdlib-only: pipeline/aw.py is NOT stdlib (numpy/PIL/scipy at its top - the prep's
  "pure stdlib" assumption is wrong for the current aw.py), so the three Mercator one-liners
  (R=6378137.0) are mirrored inline with a pointer comment; the anchor is built as the exact form
  similarity() inverts, z = a*(w-w0)+z0, a = (m_per_px/ground_scale(lat0))*e^(i theta), v down.
- Scale: bar box in blank_boxes (box presence beats the boolean) + tick-list text -> span/bar_len_px
  (bar_len = max(box w,h) in the size frame: maiden/old-sarum bars are vertical); ratio text
  ("N unit to the inch" incl. fractions/word numbers/parentheticals, "1 in = N unit", "1:NNNm",
  "1/N of nature") at BOOK_PPI -> m/px; a bar box holding ratio text (all six S&D rows) takes the
  ratio value (source "ratio"); unparseable -> never a guess -> coarse. Coarse scale =
  max(size_m)/max(panel px) (verified: aztalan +32%, serpent ~3x, arbor-low ~10x vs hand fits).
- BOOK_PPI: the five recorded ppis from data/books/summary.md; lapham_1855/squier_1851/whittlesey_1850
  stay None (web lookup this session found no published ppi for those scans) -> their ratio-only rows
  (incl. aztalan "Scale 20 ft. to an inch.") take the coarse path, the prep-sanctioned fallback.
- Rotation: north_angle_deg iff north_arrow true and |deg| <= 60, else 0 north-up with the rejected
  hint kept in note (hopeton 131.1 rejected; newark 0.0 is the missing-wrong-hint class, not rejected).
- ground: site lidar false -> "aerial", else "relief" (all 384 sites have size_m; all 276 commons rows
  have size and no map_box; all 890 book plan ids are in the index - verified this session).
- Pairs: 4 panel corners + panel centre (page centre if no map_box), in the row's size frame (book
  rows: plans size == full_size; serpent's index size null falls back to full_size), u/v 1dp,
  lon/lat 7dp - the platefit-doc convention; deterministic, no timestamps.

## 2026-10-11 202 DECISION: test-design corrections found while building (the prep's two details that do not hold)
1. The prep's "re-fit of the emitted pairs reproduces scale_m_px/rot_deg within 1e-6" CANNOT hold: the
   emitted pairs round lon/lat to 7 dp (~0.11 m), so re-fitting the ROUNDED pairs deviates from the
   anchor by at most ~1e-4 m/px absolute (measured, forthill; relative ~6e-4) and <0.05 deg rotation.
   The test uses: scale 1e-3 m/px absolute (or 2% relative), rotation 0.1 deg; the byte-exact golden
   test is the primary determinism check.
2. parse_scale corpus details (measured over all 508 scale_text values: 488 parse - 222 ratio,
   266 span - 20 None; known-bad all None): the equals-form ("scale 1 in = 240 feet") exists in the
   real corpus as "Scale: 1 in. = 100 feet" (colon after "scale", dotted "in.") and as the compound
   mills statement "1 in. = 100 ft. 1/2 in. = 50 ft. 1/4 in. = 25 ft." (first statement wins) - the
   regex was fixed for all three. "N unit to the inch" rows with a parenthetical ("2 Ch. (352 Ft.)",
   "12 Rods 200 feet") take the FIRST quantity. Optional polish left: reorder inch alternations to
   (?:inches|inch|in) in the two regexes (no current corpus value needs it).

## 2026-10-11 203a DECISION: the 001 gate is a pure-statistics one-shot - R1 resolved, adapter is thin
The prep notes (R1) inferred an ink/DEM conjunct gate; the MERGED 001 is different: fit_gate.gate(pairs,
transform=None) -> (verdict, stats {n, med, slope_o, io}) - numpy only, no images, no network
(docs/gate.md s.2). So the batch needs no fail-closed gate inputs, no DEM, no old-map ink. The adapter
fit/batch_us.py gate_placement solves plan_fit.similarity from the model's pairs, scores the claimed
placement against the recorded anchors, and records the gate's median residual (m) as the doc's score.
Caveat (by design, docs/gate.md s.7): when the placement is the least-squares fit of the same pairs the
gate sees ~0 residuals ("true case"); it rejects degenerate/nan anchor sets (n<3, no radial spread) and a
claimed placement inconsistent with its anchors. The real check on the auto fits is the human's 18/20
sample - the report lists every auto doc's median for that.

BUG FOUND, fix pending: fit/batch_us.py llm_model scale cross-check computes
`implied = abs(float(a)) * k` but `a` from plan_fit.similarity is COMPLEX (pipeline/plan_fit.py
L35-45); float(a) drops the imaginary part, so for a rotated fit implied = |s*cos(theta)|
(60 deg -> 50% low) and can misfire "not trusted" on a true answer. Fix: `implied = abs(a) * k`
(abs of a complex is the magnitude). Not applied yet - fit/batch_us.py is unmodified this
session (all analysis was /tmp throwaway; no tests run, last state 5/5 + 34/34 after 4b).

## 2026-10-11 203a DECISION: session 4d - the two pending fit/batch_us.py fixes from 4c are in: the scale cross-check uses the complex magnitude, and the feature-presence check lands; the 5-pick pilot now routes all four fabrication classes to items and pyramid (the one real fit) stays auto
4c's two pending items, implemented in fit/batch_us.py (the task's only code file; the checkout
elsewhere is untouched, and the pilot writes nothing):
1. BUG FIX: the scale cross-check was `implied = abs(float(a)) * k` but `a` from
   plan_fit.similarity is complex (scale + rotation). numpy's float() discards the imaginary part
   (ComplexWarning), so for a rotated fit implied = |s*cos(theta)|*k - measured on a real hand
   fit (mound-bottom) rotated 60 deg: old 0.5243 vs true 1.0597 m/px, a 2.02x ratio that would
   misfire "not trusted" on a TRUE answer. Now `implied = abs(a) * k` (the magnitude IS the
   scale): the same rotated fit gives exactly ratio 1.000, no misfire. The NaN guard is unchanged.
2. FEATURE-PRESENCE CHECK (the 4c decision, now code): new `_feature_present(site, pts_ll,
   gpath)` after _parse_answer. Grayscale array of the site's own ground image (PIL "L" /255);
   each answer ground point scored by the std of a 24 m box (R=12 px at 1 m/px, centre clamped
   to [R, W-R-1], the inverse of _ground_lonlat through the site corners); null = 400 boxes at
   np.random.default_rng(20261011) (distinct from the report SEED 20261010); pass iff >= half
   the points score >= the null median; ANY read failure -> (True, None) (no evidence never
   blocks; the gate and the human sample are downstream). Wired into the llm_model closure after
   the scale cross-check, only when len(pairs) >= 4; failure appends the note "only %d%% of the
   recorded points sit on more textured ground than a typical region of this site: the features
   are not visible where claimed - not trusted" and trims to 3 pairs (the <4 branch routes the
   picture to the review item). Module + llm_model docstrings document the check, its deliberate
   faint-feature over-refusal, and the known residual (fabrications anchored on strong texture
   - roads, field boundaries - pass it; docs/gate.md section 7's human 18/20 sample is the
   backstop).
3. VERIFIED (offline): _feature_present reproduces the 4c calibration on the recorded 4c points:
   tremper 1/4 -> (False, 0.25), pyramid 3/4 -> (True, 0.75); hand-fit over-refusals serpent 0/3,
   mayburgh 1/3, little-solsbury 2/5 (all genuinely faint - deliberate). Recorded-answer tests
   bypass llm_model by design, so the suite is unaffected: tests/test_batch_us.py 5/5 (5.0 s),
   full suite 34/34 in .venv (42 s).
4. PILOT RE-RUN (tunnel re-verified UP on 8080/8081; /tmp/pilot_llm.py, raw answers now

   n0636_1 -> range-validate drops the ladder to 1 pair -> item; cahokia -> scale cross-check
   (1.639 vs 3.333) trims to 3 -> item. Exactly the expected routing, no regressions.

final hand-over (the newer, authoritative word from the loop's owner) says "shared SEED
20261010", and write_report/_sample take no seed parameter - the module constant is the only

reproduces both unambiguously; 212 later draws the combined 20-sample from the union with its
own seed. No loop change requested from 203a: its seams matched the prep contract, except
run_sites' signature is (root, site_ids, model) not (root, site_ids, working, gate) - the gate is
always the real frozen one (injectable in tests by monkeypatching batch_us.gate_placement, the
pattern 203a's own test uses).

rec["pre_fit"]`, and is_pre_fit counts the old-style data/sites/<id>/site.json plate fit (kept by
design, 203a's R9). Of the 178 candidate-less EU sites, fudge/highbank/hopeton carry old-style
fits, so they end in the "already hand-fitted" bucket with no item (box 1's "or an existing hand
fit" covers them; prep note section 1's "those three become no-picture-found items" is void
against the merged loop - changing the guard would be a 203a loop edit and is not needed).
bibracte (France, zero candidates, an existing 201-era no-picture item) has its item re-upserted
with the batch's searched wording. So the full EU run is expected to: write 175 no-picture items
(174 new files + bibracte's re-save), classify 175 as "no picture found", and the remaining 119
candidate sites split between automatic fit / review item / already hand-fitted per the LLM's
answers. Dry run of the 6 region-less sample with 202's recorded proposals: all 29 candidates

c_ffb3daac218b481f row now); avebury has 6 candidates (1 pre-doc), old-sarum 5 (2 pre-docs).

## 2026-10-11 203b DECISION: report union section computed over the tree state AFTER the run; the full 384-coverage assertion is state-gated in the test
batch_eu.write_report appends union_section(union_check(root)) after batch_us.write_report wrote
the base report, so the section reflects the tree as it stands after run_sites. In the hermetic
test the temp tree only runs the sample, so that section lists the (expected) missing EU sites -
deterministic, and the test asserts the weaker form there (partitions disjoint + union = all
sites of the tree, the 6 region-less in the EU list, sample sites covered, the EU run wrote no
file naming a US site). The full assertion (0 missing, 0 in both, 0 orphans over the live
checkout) is skip-gated until the working run's report shows "Sites run: 294" - state-agnostic by
design, like 803's re-baseline: it never pins the live population to 384, because 211's merge
will grow sites.json and 212 re-covers the growth (the parent's tests/test_batch.py owns the
frozen original set). The union check keys items by the item's site FIELD (the file name's lead
plan moves under upsert) and docs by the site field with a name-prefix consistency check.

## 2026-10-11 203b DECISION: the hand-fitted-sites test went state-aware after the run made two of its pinned plans into pre-docs

The one test that failed after the working run:
test_hand_fitted_sites_get_items_for_their_other_pictures. Session 2 wrote its expectations
for the PRE-run tree: old-sarum's pinned set was its 3 fresh commons rows, but the run made
2 of them (c_041083ec68b532f8, c_ff62c9585a92b375) automatic docs, so the test's recorded run
skips them as pre-docs (never re-fitted, no entry) and the item gained no entry for them
("Extra items in the right set"). Avebury was unaffected (its 5 fresh rows all stayed item
entries, replaced in place). Fix, in my own test file only (no loop change - the skip rule is
203a's correct behaviour): pinned = short-list ∩ fresh (fresh = candidates minus pre_docs,
re-read from the tree at test time); the expected item candidate set = the live item's
candidates (a working run may have left one) ∪ fresh (exact equality, not a superset); the
lead assertion now checks the post-run invariant (exactly one file, named after
candidates[0].plan) instead of "first fresh candidate in file order"; resolved is compared
against the live item's (write-once, never set by the batch). The form is correct before AND
after any run, and under plans.json growth or 212's re-coverage run (the live item's entries
merge in, the fresh ones append). PITFALLS entry filed (recorded-answer batch tests are
state-dependent on the tree).

## 2026-10-11 204 DECISION: stage() order fixed - the date/date_commons swap runs BEFORE commons.keep() (the sources.py pattern); sanity runs green; lead doc's NL and collection sections written
The hand-over's step 1 predicted `stage --dry` would print "staged: 13 new sites, 13 new pictures" - it instead
raised on the Sluis 1980 RCE-album reproduction: stage() evaluated commons.keep() on the RAW page metadata
before the known-drawing-date swap, and keep() judges via year_of() from m["date"] (1980 > MAX_YEAR 1925 ->
drop). The pattern it was copied from (sources.py run()) swaps first and gates second: the done Zwolle row is
the proof (date "circa 1560", date_commons "1980", the same album family) - a post-1925 reproduction of a
c. 1550 drawing must pass, judged on the drawing's year. My ordering was a deviation, now fixed (swap moved
before keep(), with the comment); the Rotterdam TOWNS comment was corrected too (the page's date is the
year-1563 field, one year of the drawing's 1558-1563 window - not an upload timestamp). stage --dry now
green: "staged: 13 new sites, 13 new pictures" (the 13 slugs + the build list; transcript
/tmp/vd_stage_dry_iter44.txt); --check exit 0 (staging files still absent: joins empty, 0 evidence rows);
suite 33/33; --list passed again (fifth live run of 2026-10-11, after the town_files extension). Lead doc:

misfiled-in-Sluis note; (c) The collection categories section written: BNE 61 (the "Planos de ciudades"
atlas plates - all 23 FR towns + 16 of the 20 BE towns + the done Binche/Venlo; the Aire+Thérouanne and

## 2026-10-11 204 DECISION: cat_chain fixed for the current MediaWiki (prop=categories, fail-loud on warnings) + 60-fetch cap + CHAINS table - 56 walks launched
The first `--chain` probe of the long run (Ghent / Brussels / Luxembourg City) exposed a broken walker: `cat_chain`
fetched a category's parents with `action=query&prop=categoryinfo&cicategories=1&ciclimit=500`, but the current
MediaWiki API does not recognize those parameters (response carries `warnings.main.*: "Unrecognized parameters:
cicategories, ciclimit."` - and `commons.get` returns the body without surfacing the warning). So every walk saw
ZERO parents and silently "failed" in ~1 s per town (even Ghent, which the 2026-10-10 prep measured as holding at
4 hops). Two fixes in fit/src_van_deventer.py (my own file):
- **`prop=categories` + `cllimit=500`** for a category page's CONTAINING categories (verified live: Ghent's van
  Deventer category returns '16th-century maps of Ghent' + 'Maps by Jacob van Deventer'), plus a fail-loud guard:
  any response with `error` or `warnings` -> SystemExit. The silent zero-parents degradation is exactly the case
  prep R7 warned about ("the walk is the verification" - a walk that fetches nothing verifies nothing).
- **`CHAIN_FETCH_CAP = 60` per walk + per-level progress lines** (the transcript gets `level k: queue N, next M,
  fetches F` per town). Measured chains are 3-5 hops and a few dozen fetches (Ghent holds at 38), but the BFS
  expands ALL parents, so a disambiguated p373 the map tree does not hold within 6 hops explodes the frontier
  (Brussels (municipality): 730 s and the full 6-level frontier pre-fix, still no hit - genuinely absent, the map
  tree uses the plain 'Brussels' name). The cap bounds that town to ~a minute and sends it to the caption route.
  Refinement found by the probe: when the target was added to the visited set by a REAL fetch before the cap
  stopped the pop loop, the `via[target]` path is fully walked (every edge was returned by an API response), so
  it is returned when it is within the depth cap - that is how Luxembourg City's 4-hop chain is recovered from a
  capped walk. The final message now distinguishes "sits N hops out, beyond the depth cap" / "capped at 60
  fetches" / "no chain within 6 hops".
- **The recording home moved from an inline TOWNS "chain" key to a module-level `CHAINS` table** (the stub comment
  after TOWNS that existed for exactly this) plus a `chain_of(town)` helper used by `evidence_for` and `stage`.
  Rationale: the read tool has not been byte-faithful on this .py file (PITFALLS) and 56 multi-line dict edits
  would be fragile; one mechanical table is reviewable. TOWNS header comment, the cat_chain docstring and the
  stub comment all document the new home; an inline "chain" is still honoured (chain_of checks it first).
Probe results (post-fix, capped): Ghent HOLDS 4 hops via 'Historical images of Ghent' (a different middle than the
prep's 'Maps of Ghent' - both real, this is the actually-walked one); Luxembourg City HOLDS 4 hops via
'Geography of Luxembourg City'; Brussels CAPPED -> caption route (file title names the town). The full 56-town
walk runs in the background (transcript /tmp/vd_chains_iter55.txt, PID file /tmp/vd_chains_iter55.pid); the CHAINS
table gets filled from the transcript's `record:` lines next session after an eyeball pass of every path against
its P373.

```python
]

inv = []
used = set()
problems = []
for k, v, rs in R:
    hits = [t for t in rows if k in t and t not in used]
    if not hits:
        problems.append(f"rule matches nothing: {k}")
    for t in hits:
        if t in used:
            problems.append(f"double-assigned: {t!r} by rule {k!r}")
        used.add(t)
        inv.append((t, rows[t]["w"], rows[t]["h"], v, rs))
for t in rows:
    if t not in used:
        problems.append(f"UNMATCHED: {t}")
from collections import Counter
vc = Counter(x[3] for x in inv)
print("verdict counts:", dict(vc), "total:", len(inv))
for p in problems:
    print("PROBLEM:", p)
for t, w, h, v, rs in inv:
    if v == "staged" and max(w, h) < 1000: problems.append(f"staged but small: {t}")
    if v == "drop: too small" and max(w, h) >= 1000: problems.append(f"too-small but big: {t}")
if problems:
    raise SystemExit(1)

def q(s):
    return repr(s)
lines = [f"    ({q(t)}, {w}, {h}, {q(v)}, {q(rs)})," for t, w, h, v, rs in sorted(inv)]
block = "INVENTORY = [\n" + "\n".join(lines) + "\n]\n"
path = "fit/src_kip.py"
src = open(path).read()
old = 'INVENTORY = []  # filled by the first live run (session 1); every title of the lead categories, once'
assert src.count(old) == 1
src = src.replace(old, block)
old_doc = "(an empty INVENTORY only lists - the first run builds it)"
assert src.count(old_doc) == 1
src = src.replace(old_doc, "(the committed 132-row table; a renamed, re-filed or replaced file fails loud)")
open(path, "w").write(src)
print("spliced into", path)
```

## 2026-10-11 206 DECISION: the p.212 plate is Volume 2, not bracketed by Oxford p.207 as the task framing said
    The live file description (verified 2026-10-11) says "Volume : 02, Page : 212" for p.212 while the p.207
    Oxford row says "Volume : 01": the two IA volumes paginate separately, so a file number is a leaf number
    WITHIN its volume. The task's "p.207 Oxford / p.231 Lancaster bracket it" is only half true: p.212 and
    p.231 are both V2, 19 leaves apart, and the plate sits in V2's Cheshire section (the V2 chapter text and
    index name Shotwick: "Shotwick, ii. 180"). The lead doc must record the true layout (OQ-3: the actual
    county headings in the V2 OCR, from archive.org castlesofengland02mack hocr) - it still constrains the
    plate, just not the way the task file phrases it.

### 2026-10-11 206 DECISION: the p.212 plan is printed NORTH-UP (page top = north), not north-left as the arrow suggests; the surviving ring is the C11 motte
    Two findings from measuring the fetched page against the ground (details in the task hand-over): (1) The book's p.212 drawing (pentagon, five towers, hall, dashed building, gate) sits in the page's top-left quadrant (bbox ~(10,0)-(425,425) of the 839x840 frame). The only clear surviving ring on the LiDAR/aerial is a ~40 m ditched mound at (E +0..+11 m, N -46..-48 m) from the catalog point - the size and ditch match the C11 MOTTE (Gatehouse 212: 40x20 m hexagonal mound, 25 m ditch), not a 12 m castle tower. With the three reliable anchors (plan hall -> the bright building cluster NW of centre; plan dashed building -> the speck W; plan T4 (bottom horseshoe) -> the motte) the solved similarity under the printed orientation (page left = N, per the arrow) is rotated ~90 deg off the ground, while under north-up it comes out with rotation ~0 deg, scale ~0.41 m/px, the pentagon's south corner due south on the motte and the hall on the NW building: the plan is a normal north-up map and the printed left-pointing arrow is 90 deg off (or marks something else - /tmp/arrow_crop.jpg, 4x zoom of page (170,235)-(345,290), to be re-read; /tmp may be gone). CAVEAT recorded for the fit: with exactly 3 pairs any similar triangle fits both conventions to ~0 residual (6 eqs, 4 params) - the final doc needs >= 4 pairs so the gate is not degenerate.

### 2026-10-11 206 DECISION: the book's own V2 text identifies the plate's castle (OQ-3 resolved); the plan is the 1600 Randle Holme drawing, a pentagon with SIX towers, five drawn
    Re-downloaded the V2 OCR (archive.org castlesofengland02mack: hocr searchtext/pageindex + page_numbers; the page_numbers file is format v2 {identifier, format-version, confidence, pages:[{leafNum, pageNumber}]} and pageindex rows are [leafStart, leafEnd, textStart, textEnd] - the prep's "leaf->printed map" guess was wrong). The Shotwick chapter (printed p.180, running head CHESHIRE 181 on the continuation page; next chapter STOCKPORT) says: the plan is "a drawing and plan of this fortress by Kendle [Randle] Holme, as it appeared then, in a ruinous condition. Its trace was a pentagon strengthened by six towers, one of which, according to Camden, was five storeys high", and of the 1897 remains: "those seen at present consisting merely of a large mound, supported by a huge earthwork of crescent form, and two deep entrenchments on the land side". The index reads "Shotlack, ii. 179, Shotwick, ii. 180". So the p.212 plate (leaf 212 = printed 164; the Lancaster plate leaf 231 = printed 183, 19 pages on) is a plan of Shotwick drawn from Holme's c.1600 depiction of the ruined fortress - which explains the five (of six) towers, the thick bank, and why the modern ground only carries the motte + crescent + entrenchments. This is the backbone of docs/lead_mackenzie.md section 3 (evidence chain a-f) and of the fit's expectations.

### 2026-10-11 206 DECISION: iter-58 page measurements are VOID - the p.212 drawing spans the whole page, not the top-left quadrant; corrected page map recorded
Iter 58's coordinates (T1 (195,55) ... hall (225,168) ... crop (10,0,425,425)) came from a 1678 px "2x" sheet that the agent view rendered shrunken; I anchored the grid labels to the wrong place and measured a phantom half-size pentagon. Fresh evidence this session: a dark-pixel census of the RAW 839x840 scan (10 px row bands, merged x-runs) plus four 3x quadrant crops with 25 px grid labels (recipe /tmp/page_quads.py) show the pentagonal earthwork occupying essentially the whole page. Corrected map (page px): vertices TOP (405,25), RIGHT (827,570), BOTTOM (620,835), LOWER-LEFT (30,690), LEFT (50,430); five horseshoe C towers on the bank edges at approx (185,195) [opens right], (725,235) [opens lower-left], (795,560) [left - confirm on pg_BR], (640,760) [opens upper-left], (105,680) [opens right]; HALL thin-line building (430-525, 185-305) + right wing (centre (475,245)); small dashed building (255-360, 575-750) (centre (300,660)); thin NORTH ARROW: horizontal line y approx 472 x approx 355-605, vertical crossbar x approx 455 (y 425-510, small filled wedge/monogram at (445-455,462) - unread at 6x, crop (430,420)-(480,520) at 8x to read it), arrowhead at the LEFT end (points west); GATE GAP in the thick bank at approx (300-335, 745-765) on the lower-left edge (census-verified gap); no caption or scale bar anywhere (full-page census). The north-up orientation conclusion of iter 58 stands in principle (re-derive rotation from the corrected pairs). The iter-58 "P2 motte at (E +0..+11, N -46..-48)" ground read was made on a 900 px sheet with the 4x-label bug and is likewise NOT trustworthy - re-identify the motte on corrected 10 m-grid quadrants (book: 40x20 m mound + crescent bank + two land-side entrenchments).

### 2026-10-11 206 DECISION: 800 m and 200 m Esri crops are the same ground (approx 4 m tile-rounding offset); a throwaway 800 m sheet had a flipped-label bug
Cross-correlating the centre 400 px of the 800 m aerial with the 200 m zoom crop (both 800x800) peaks at a (+15,+15) px offset approx 4 m (r=0.43, smooth ramp) - same ground, different composites (Esri tile rounding), not a centre mismatch. So the 200 m crop (0.25 m/px) is the authoritative fit-session image; the 800 m sheet is context only (features are sub-pixel in it: 800 m crops of the building/mound candidates showed plain grass). Also: a throwaway /tmp/mack_800.py swapped y-values when unpacking aw.corners() ([TL,TR,BR,BL] => to_merc(TL).y is the NORTH edge, to_merc(BR).y the SOUTH), printing N/S-flipped latitude labels on that one sheet; the module's zoom() (centre +/- h) is immune and verified (centre label -2.97608,53.22720).

### 2026-10-11 206 DECISION: EA LiDAR (environment.data.gov.uk WCS) is flaky under request bursts - 500s or silent all-zeros after about 15 calls in a session
At session start the 800 m box returned 640000/640000 px (elevations 3.8-25.3 m, sane for the Dee estuary) and the 200 m crop worked twice; after a dozen more requests every size (200/400/800 m) returned HTTP 500 (6 retries, all failed) or, earlier, a silent all-zero array. In aw.fetch_dem an all-zero EA answer is no-data, and with no static S3 source for Cheshire the 800 m path then RAISES. Next session: space the DEM calls (one at a time, minutes apart) and guard relief rendering against NaN/all-zero (nanmin/nanmax; a NaN array renders as a black image - that is how /tmp/rqNW|SE.jpg first came out).

<!-- task 213 -->
# Task 213 artist-views

## 2026-10-11 213 DECISION: staged rows use hand-recorded caption_coords evidence; record --live is the wrong route (traps confirmed live)
`fit/evidence.py:source_tables()` only reads `pipeline/sources.py:SOURCES`, and fit/evidence.py is not in my
Touches, so a `hand_tied` row for "fit/src_artists.py" would FAIL check. I hand-record the `caption_coords`
field in caption_route's key order (rec/kind/checked/named_place/place_qid/place_label/place_lon/place_lat/
site_lon/site_lat/dist_m). `record` skips rows that already carry rec==evidence/1, so 211's later record runs
leave them alone. Prep OQ-1 (a byte-identical live record for Venice) is unachievable: `record --live` resolves
the caption by NAME SEARCH, and --verify confirmed the first hit with P625 is the trap for BOTH sites: search
"Imola" -> Q50195 itself, which carries the bad P625 (11.7147, 44.3531), 53 km NW -> conflict -> kind none;
search "Venice" -> Q773853 (Venice, Los Angeles, -118.46, 33.99), 9891 km -> conflict -> kind none. The hand
record stores the verified resolution instead (Imola: old-town centre as both site and place; Venice: its own
P625, which is sane). Also verified live 2026-10-11: labels 'Imola'/'Venice' (enwiki sitelinks), P373
'Imola'/'Venice' (so the row's `cat` field is the P373 category, though the file categories don't chain into
it -> caption_coords), P131 = provinces (Imola: Q16139 Province of Bologna + Q18288145 Metropolitan City of
Bologna - the prep misread Q16139 as Italy; Italy is Q38; Venice: four Venice-province variants Q6360367/
Q3924689/Q16310/Q3678587) -> the extras `place` strings are composed by hand (region, country). File metadata
confirmed: RCIN 912284 = 2000x1483, no DateTimeOriginal and no description, year 1502 from the title (year_of
agrees with the table's date, so no date/date_commons override fires), public domain; Correr Venice =
11800x5856, DateTimeOriginal "1500 ... /9", public domain. Both pass commons.keep().

## 2026-10-11 213 DECISION: new-site region is "Italy" (display case); filed task 214 for REGION_NAME
REGION_NAME (build_catalog.py) has no "italy" key. `place_of` uses `.get(x, x)` (the display-case "Italy"
dedupes cleanly in the place string; lowercase "italy" would print lowercase), but `make_site` uses
`REGION_NAME.get(...) or "US"` - until the key exists, the built sites display as "US" on the page. Task 214
filed (one module: pipeline/build_catalog.py + new tests/test_region.py; Depends on: none, so it can run in
parallel with the rest of 213).

## 2026-10-11 213 DECISION: the "Google Art Project" scans are documented drops, not staged
commons.NO's regex contains "google" (a harvest heuristic against map-screenshot junk), so both GAp scans fail
commons.keep() - and even if staged they would be dead rows: 211's merge feeds commons.rows(), which re-judges
every row with keep() and would drop them from the plan list. Instead I stage the surviving scans (RCIN 912284
for Imola; the 11800x5856 Museo Correr scan for Venice). Also to be documented as drops in the lead doc: the
NGA "block" files (detail crops of Barbari's woodcut, not separate pictures), File:...RCIN 912686 (a different
work: recto sketches), File:Imola 1705.jpg (a 1705 view, not Leonardo), and File:Leonardo da vinci, Town plan
of Imola.jpg (triage pending session 2 - possibly a third scan of the same sheet).

## 2026-10-11 213 DECISION: module shape - SITES table + per-source row tuples, stdlib-only at the top
fit/src_artists.py: SITES{qid: site facts (id/name/lon/lat/place/region/kind/wikipedia/cat/p625/note)};
SOURCES{name: rows + display label + p-field} for the two image-level sources (row tuple (title, qid, label,
opts) - a variant of sources.py: the region lives on the site, since all its rows share it); BOOKS{civitates,
merian} with the IA identifiers (per-book flow is session 4; the books.py:BOOKS entry is a shared-module
change -> file a task, never write it). Top-level imports are stdlib only (json/os/sys/time); commons/evidence
are imported lazily inside functions because commons->aw needs numpy (absent from the system python3) - live
commands run under .venv/bin/python. CLI: --verify (live QID re-check, prints the search traps next to the
table values for the lead doc), [--dry] (stage), --check (offline join + evidence.check over the staged rows;
books arg = a never-existing path), --golden (normalized staged rows -> tests/golden/src_artists_sample.json,
checked/date_commons zeroed). check_staged() join: file rows may name staged OR existing sites (box-3 rule:
existing QID -> picture row on the existing site id, no new catalog entry); staged ids/QIDs must not pre-exist;
classified rows 1:1 with file rows.

## 2026-10-11 213 DECISION: the prep's Imola "centre 44.245 N, 12.367 E / bad P625 53 km NW" was wrong - that point is Cervia; P625 is the correct city point
The prep notes placed Imola's old town at 44.245 N, 12.367 E and, seeing Q50195's P625 (44.353, 11.715) far
from it, called the P625 "bad, ~53 km NW of the town". Verified live (2026-10-11): the prep point is CERVIA
on the Ravenna coast (~50 km SE of Imola); the real city is at 44.353 N, 11.715 E, where THREE independent
sources agree: the enwiki infobox (44°21'11"N 11°42'53"E), Wikidata P625, and the OSM town node (with the F1
circuit beside it and the Santerno - "Fiume Santerno" at 44.3606/11.7104 - running through the urban area).
So the "53 km trap" was the distance between two wrong values. Consequences: (a) the site point is the
walled town's centre, 11.7112/44.3535 (Piazza del Duomo, OSM) - the centre of the sheet's circular view, not
the modern city point; (b) the prep's "Savena crossing" was a second error - the drawing's river is the
Santerno, which Leonardo labels "Sant'Arno" (mirror script, read off the sheet); the view is south-up per
the literature; (c) the evidence story changes: for Imola the live record route resolves the RIGHT QID (the
hand record still stands to pin the walled centre, deterministic and offline); the name-search trap that is
REAL and documented is Venice (first hit with P625 = Q773853 Venice, California, 9891 km). Side note: OSM's
comune boundary for Imola is mis-drawn (Nominatim displays the urban area as "Imola, Nuovo Circondario
Imolese", and the admin relation's centre sits 8 km north of the city) - a reason never to geocode Imola
from OSM/Nominatim. The PITFALLS entry "Q50195 (Imola) carries a bad P625..." must be corrected accordingly
(pending in the next session; I cannot edit PITFALLS from the context-limit hand-over step).

## 2026-10-11 213 DECISION: iteration 25's orphaned triage process kept appending after the driver's snapshot commit
The driver's "uncommitted work after iteration 25" commit captured civitatesorbiste00brau.A.jsonl at 191
lines; the orphaned run kept appending until 04:03 (211 pages, n 0-210 complete, no error rows) and exited.

error). Lesson: before committing (or trusting) a data file a background job may still be writing, check

mid-growth states. (Recorded here because the context-limit step allowed only DECISIONS/tasks edits; the
next agent files the PITFALLS entry.)

## 2026-10-11 213 DECISION: list structure decided - one entry per figure leaf keyed on leaf n, title = the plate's own inscription or the section's title leaf, kind o/s/u with kind_by hand|A
The book has no printed town list (confirmed: no list leaf in the A data). Each city section = a TITLE
LEAF (big Latin title + history text; A reads it) + 1-3 PLATE leaves (views/plans; A caption = the
plate's own inscription, often a legend or poem, often empty), interleaved both ways, so a plate's city =
its section (nearest title leaf either side). Printed page numbers exist ONLY on leaves 193-196 (1-4)
and 280-284 (I-V) (data/raw/books/civitatesorbiste00brau/civitatesorbiste00brau_page_numbers.json) - all
other `page` values null. Plan for data/books/lists/civitatesorbiste00brau.json: {"plates": null,
"figures": [{"fig": <0-based leaf n>, "title": <inscription as printed, or the city name from the section
title leaf when the plate has none>, "page": null-or-printed, "kind": "o"|"s"|"u",
"kind_by": "hand" (I read the image) or "A" (derived from stage A, unverified)}]} covering all 121 figure
leaves; .map.json = [] (list_pages maps only kind s/u and has no printed page numbers for these leaves,
so it is honestly empty - 217 extends the machinery, not 213). Visually confirmed so far: plans
n58/85/90/98/109/237/238 (s); views n194/202/214/217/218/225/157/158 (o). Remaining reads (cheap - the

# Task 300 site-stonehenge

## 2026-10-11 300 DECISION: the one-site line (tools/site_done.py) reuses batch_us.run_sites only for UNCOVERED candidates, and needs three root-aware refactors (plan_fit, build_site, scoped review.export)
Where: tools/site_done.py (new), pipeline/plan_fit.py, pipeline/build_site.py, fit/review.py
Rationale: measured state - Stonehenge's 13 plans.json rows all have a verdict (7 auto docs, 6 in its
review item, sets disjoint), so running run_sites blindly (even model=None) would not fit anything new
but its write_item upsert would REPLACE the 6 rich batch-written candidate entries (gate scores, model
pairs, why) with "no model answer" stubs (upsert merges by whole-entry replacement, PITFALLS fit/).
So site_done's fit step runs run_sites only when a candidate has neither doc nor item entry (or the
site has no candidates at all, no pre-fit and no item - the no-picture case). The existing steps were
not root-aware: plan_fit and build_site derive every path from their own file location, and
review.export records the write-once resolved field into EVERY derivably-resolved item (today exactly
one other site: alligator-effigy-mound-q4732645) - a one-site command must not touch other sites'
files, hence export(root, site_id=None) (scoped record, same page file) and _root(root=None) threading
through plan_fit (build/main/prune/load_picture + new is_done) and build_site (drawings/set_drawings/
write_entry). serve.py calls plan_fit.main([]) - default args must stay behaviour-identical.
Also fixed in plan_fit while in there: the `how` string claims "Placed by hand" for automatic fits
(wrong on the public page; auto docs get "Fitted automatically by the batch ... (gate score X m)",
hand-fit strings unchanged), and load_picture's bare urllib has no Wikimedia pacing/Retry-After
(7 back-to-back downloads 429, PITFALLS ~L602) - new _fetch helper paces ~1 rps, honours Retry-After,
backoffs on 5xx. Baseline before all changes: 59 passed / 0 failed (59.76 s). Not yet implemented at
hand-over: the design is fully specced in the task's Hand-over; next session writes the code, then the
real run (7 Commons downloads expected), then a re-run for idempotency, then tests/browser/docs.

## 2026-10-11 300 DECISION: the one-site line is implemented and has taken Stonehenge to the map; one session remains (tests/test_site_done.py)
Where: tools/site_done.py (new), pipeline/plan_fit.py, pipeline/build_site.py, fit/review.py, docs/site_flow.md, docs/site_flow/stonehenge.png, web/data/{sites.json,review.json,sites/stonehenge-q39671/}, data/raw/commons/big/

`tree with no model flag. Results, all verified on disk: (1) fit - "13 candidate picture(s): 7 fitted (doc),
6 in the review item -> no batch run needed" (the covered/uncovered distinction worked: run_sites was never
called, so no item entry was clobbered); (2) sync - 7 documents synced, each big picture downloaded from

and rendered in red ink exactly on the real sarsen circles over the aerial photograph; screenshot committed
as docs/site_flow/stonehenge.png with its one-line description in docs/site_flow.md.
What broke this session (all fixed, all documented in docs/site_flow.md for the next site): the entry line
