<!-- task 301 -->
# Task 301 site-argentoratum

## 2026-10-11 301 DECISION: lead redirect - Stonehenge is not done first; its 7 batch fits are all fabricated frame-to-box identity maps, and the gate passed them by construction
The lead (mid-session, 2026-10-11): before Argentoratum, check each of Stonehenge's 7 synced drawings; anything visibly off goes to the review queue with the reason; find why the gate passed the Rijksmuseum plan (16.7 m, 2-3x too big, off-centre) and make the LINE catch it, with a test; correct the false sentence in docs/site_flow.md; then Argentoratum.
Root cause (verified this session): all 7 stonehenge auto docs (c_0226 23.2 m, c_098c 7.4 m, c_20f9 44.1 m, c_6933 26.8 m, c_83b4 16.7 m, c_897a 38.0 m, c_f838 0.0 m) are the model's documented fabrication class (batch_us.llm_model docstring, pilots 4a/4c: "internally perfect regular patterns (identity maps, corner-to-corner, diagonal ladders)"): regular ladders of round page-px coordinates (u 200-1080 step ~200-400, v 150-1200 step ~100-200) mapped to regular ground ladders (points on shared lon/lat lines 100-450 m from the site centre; 4 of the 7 have one anchor within ~1 m of the site's NOMINAL centre - the catalog coordinate, not a feature), implied scale 0.80-1.07 m/px = box width / page px (the model treated the page frame as the 1000x1000 m site box). Why the gate passed it: run_sites never asks the model for a placement - it SOLVES the similarity from the model's own pairs and the gate scores that least-squares fit against the same points, so the residuals are only the ladder's ~20-25% anisotropy + jitter (med 0-44 m < 45, slope_o 0.02-0.12 < 0.145, no radial pattern) -> ACCEPT. That is gate.md section 7's registered limit verbatim ("a consistent global error passes"; the gate does not re-verify the anchors are the right features) - the gate did its registered job. The batch's three pre-gate counterchecks all pass BY CONSTRUCTION on this class: the stated-scale cross-check compares the model's stated scale with its own ladder's implied scale (they agree by construction), and _feature_present passes because ladder points scattered over structureless Salisbury Plain sit at/above the 400-point null median (the next-door candidate c_ffb3d drew the 25% verdict). All 7 verified visually (composites over relief.jpg, /tmp/stonehenge_overlay_check.png): none sits on the sarsens - c_83b4 is 2-3x too big AND ~70 m off-centre (the lead's exact description), the other six likewise off in scale and/or position. Consequence the lead accepts: all 13 of Stonehenge's candidates end in the review item (human's drag is the last word) and the site shows 0 drawings until a human fits at least one.

## 2026-10-11 301 DECISION: the line's fix - a fabrication-pattern check (2D lattice / 1D ladder / frame-identity) before sync; pattern docs go to the review item with the reason, never to the map
Chosen over extending the frozen gate (that would void the gate.md registration and test_gate pins its constants) and over re-running the batch (the batch's pre-gate checks are exactly what passed by construction; the line is the last gate before the map and re-validates each site's docs on its turn - no batch-wide re-run needed). Shape (detailed in the task hand-over): a new pure function in pipeline/plan_fit.py, `fabrication_pattern(pairs, crop, size, box) -> None | reason`, three conditions any one of which fires: (a) 2D lattice (>=3 distinct u AND >=3 distinct v, consecutive gaps near-arithmetic on both axes, >=4 anchors) - fires on c_098c, c_83b4; (b) 1D ladder (>=3 anchors collinear with near-arithmetic spacing) - fires on c_0226; (c) frame-identity (the solved similarity maps the crop frame onto the site box: centre within 25% of the box side per axis, size ratio per axis in [0.7, 1.4]) - fires on c_20f9, c_6933, c_897a, c_f838. site_done._sync runs it before any download; on a hit: no build, prune any existing overlay (plan_fit.prune), upsert a review-item candidate carrying the doc's pairs + gate score + the pattern reason, recompute the entry and republish the queue. Tolerances must be validated to fire on all 7 stonehenge docs and on NONE of the 20 hand-fit docs (the golden reference). Test: extend tests/test_site_done.py in its existing hermetic harness (socket-dead child, tmp copy). Then run the line on stonehenge (no model needed: all 13 candidates covered -> no batch run, no network: pattern docs are rejected before any fetch), fix site_flow.md + replace stonehenge.png, then argentoratum.

### 2026-10-11 301 DECISION: the line's fabrication check = standalone grid-roundness plus 2D-lattice and 1D-ladder clauses on the doc's page-px pairs; frame-identity dropped, so the function takes pairs only
Chosen of the two options the previous hand-over left open: standalone grid-roundness as the primary clause (its own recommendation: a strict gate that over-refuses is fine, the human's drag is the last word, and it fires on ALL 8 argentoratum docs, not just the 5 the frame-identity+round variant caught). Frame-identity is dropped entirely: it fires on 4 legitimate hand maps (avebury/bergues/calleva/camp-d "the drawing covers the site box"; rotation cannot separate them, tested last session) and adds no coverage (roundness already fires all 15 targets). With frame-identity gone, crop/size/box are dead params, so the implemented signature is fabrication_pattern(pairs) in pipeline/plan_fit.py (pure numpy, no download; runs on the doc's stored pairs in the picture's size frame, before any fetch). (b) is kept because it is not redundant: pontefract c_3213 (22% round) is a 1D ladder - the union of the three clauses fires on EVERY batch auto doc in the repo (110/110 in data/db/platefits), not just the 15 targets.
Validation (the real function, run this session): fires 15/15 stonehenge + argentoratum auto docs - c_098c fires all three (9/10 per-axis-distinct round, lattice gaps 1.091/0.909 u + 1.125/0.893 v, two 3-anchor ladders) - and fires 110/110 repo batch docs; fires 0/20 hand fits (tests/golden/hand_fit_docs.sha256). Nearest hand margins: (a) whitley max/median 1.3066 vs 1.3, (b) castle-rising min/median 0.801 vs 0.9, (c) maiden 0.43 vs 0.9. The previous hand-over's c_098c "9/10" was per-axis-distinct (u distinct + v distinct); union-distinct would be 7/8 = 0.875 and would NOT fire (c) on c_098c - the implementation uses per-axis, the convention that lets (c) alone fire all 15. The 10-px roundness: the fit sheet's grid labels sit at 100/200/500/1000 source px (fit/batch_us._step/_grid), so a model reading labels lands on multiples of 10 px; the page tool records a human's drag with one decimal. The check is independent of the batch's three pre-gate checks (re-read llm_model/_parse_answer/_feature_present: range drop, stated-scale, texture - none look at page-px coordinate patterns).
Suite: 60 passed after the change (the function is unused until the next step wires it into site_done._sync, so nothing else could break).

## 2026-10-11 301 DECISION: the fabrication check is wired into the line (tools/site_done.py:_sync, run BEFORE any build/download); the line took Stonehenge to the map as 0 drawings / 13 review candidates; two line defects found and fixed in the line (scoped prune, stale item re-read)
Where: tools/site_done.py, pipeline/plan_fit.py (prune), data/db/platefits/stonehenge-q39671.* (7 deleted), data/review/stonehenge-*, web/data/{sites.json,review.json}
Rationale: _sync runs plan_fit.fabrication_pattern(doc pairs) on every doc before the is_done/build branch; on a hit it (1) upserts the candidate into the site's item via a new _pattern_entry (plan/title/url/pairs/evidence/gate_score, why = "line check: <reason>; the gate's X m measures internal consistency of the anchors, not their truth"), (2) deletes the doc file (a later batch run re-fits the picture; plan_fit.prune keeps no overlay of it), (3) prints "sent to the review queue (line check: ...)" and never counts it synced; after the loop the line calls plan_fit.prune(root, sid). _sync now returns (failed, sent), and main re-reads the item AFTER the sync (the upsert is invisible to _fit's snapshot) so _review/_summary/resolved all see the new candidates. The real run (no model, no network): 13 rows all covered -> no batch run; all 7 stonehenge docs hit the check; the 7 already-built plan webps + dep entries pruned; drawings 7 -> 0; item 6 -> 13 (the 6 originals byte-identical, order kept, lead unchanged); review.json republished (339 open of 340); second run idempotent (only review.json's "at" changes). Two defects the run exposed, both fixed in the line per the task's "fix in the line" box: (1) prune was a GLOBAL sweep and dropped a stale orphan dep entry of alligator-effigy-mound (a plan overlay referencing a doc that no longer exists in the store - pre-existing since before this task; the dep dir is gitignored/local, the images pipeline-rebuild, and the module's own next global prune would have dropped it anyway) - so plan_fit.prune gained a `site` parameter (site=None keeps the CLI's global sweep) and the line prunes only its own site; (2) _review printed the stale pre-sync item (6 candidates instead of 13) because it used _fit's snapshot - main re-reads after the sync. Suite 60 passed after all of it (hand-fit golden green). test_site_done.py now runs VACUOUSLY against the new repo state (0 docs) - the golden-seeded rewrite that makes it re-verify the check is the next step, after the docs step. The 7 deleted docs live on in git history (this commit's parent): git show <parent>:data/db/platefits/stonehenge-q39671.<plan>.json.

## 2026-10-11 301 DECISION: test_site_done.py re-seeds the Stonehenge fabrication incident from the recovered goldens - the line's pre-sync check is re-verified on every run
Chosen of the options the hand-over left (golden-seeded rewrite vs extending to a new site vs lighter assertions): golden-seeded rewrite with the STRONGEST pin available - the upserted item must be BYTE-IDENTICAL to the repo's current 13-candidate item, which forces the check function, the 200-evidence row lookup, the upsert's ordering/serialization, and the golden docs all to reproduce the live run exactly. Shape: (1) the 7 deleted docs recovered verbatim from git (b84d147^) into tests/golden/stonehenge_linecheck_docs/ under their original names (Touches already listed tests/golden/); (2) loud preconditions before seeding - the repo holds no stonehenge docs, the item is exactly 7 "line check:" + 6 batch candidates, fabrication_pattern still fires on each golden doc AND the recorded entry equals SITE_DONE._pattern_entry(doc, pattern, repo) (drift in the check, a golden doc, or a files.jsonl evidence row fails before anything is seeded), plans.json's 13 rows == 7+6, the published page matches the item; (3) the tree copies code + full queue + whole doc store + data/commons/files.jsonl, seeds the 7 docs, and rewinds the item to its 6 batch candidates (the upsert only appended, so removal restores the batch's file); the run asserts the ENTIRE output line by line, byte-identity of item/depictions/sites.json (0 drawings), the 7 docs deleted, every other site's files untouched, and second-run idempotency (only review.json's "at"). Two design findings: the old tree omitted data/commons/files.jsonl, which _pattern_entry reads for the evidence field - without the copy the upserted item cannot be byte-identical; and no flat-colour big-image seeding is needed at all, because a pattern doc never reaches load_picture (if the check ever stops firing, the run tries to download and dies on the socket guard). The test loads tools/site_done.py via importlib under the unique name "site_done_under_test" (its own sys.path inserts resolve batch_us/build_site/plan_fit/review; fit/ and pipeline/ have no module-name collisions, so the later test_src_* files in the same pytest process are unaffected). ## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: Clifford's Tower has P625 (6 m) - the part route adds 40 passes; part-without-P625 falls back to the site's own coords (logged, not a loosening)

Design decision (logged per the iter-14 rule "do not invent a looser substitute without logging it"):
for a part WITHOUT P625, part_mention uses the site's own coordinates - the catalog's has_part means the
part is spatially inside the site, i.e. at the site's point (0 km). No currently-none row names such a
part, so this is a safety net, not a coverage driver; the 50 km check still applies wherever the part
carries real P625.

ids dropped from the batch response (the run-1 count was probably a transient server quirk, not
reproducible). Not filed as a pitfall (unproven); the new part prefetch re-fetches ids missing from a

## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: offline candidate prototype done - extractor rules finalized (NFKD mandatory; pairs-before-singles order; title-only-pass rejected)
Iter 16 (agent c). Built /tmp/sheet_proto.py (v1) and /tmp/sheet_proto2.py (v2, throwaway, no repo
changes) and eyeballed ALL 354 stored-none rows per site against the iter-14 census. Findings that
changed the iter-15 design:
1. NFKD-normalize BEFORE tokenizing - mandatory. v1 tokenized raw text: "Orléans" -> "O"+"rl"+"ans"
   (zero candidates), which would have cost the 4 cenabum pass rows; "Bethléem"/"Maastricht" variants
   likewise. Candidates are stored in the normalized form (the check's norm-substring test stays
   valid because check() normalizes both sides).
2. Strip the "File:" prefix and truncate desc at a word boundary at 600 chars (a cut word like
   "Bethl" becomes a phantom candidate otherwise). Digits are never kept tokens (v1 bug: "000",
   "1870" leaked and formed junk pairs).
3. Run splitting: a comma OR a real lowercase word (len>=3) between two kept words breaks the run.
   Keeps "New Orleans" / "Cranbourne Hampshire" / "Monmouth Castle" as phrases while breaking OS
   place lists (v1's greedy 3-merge turned them into unresolvable garbage and lost the singles).
4. Candidate order per run: 2-word pairs, then 3-word phrases, then singles. Decisive: the lone
   "Orleans" resolves to Orléans (the cenabum site itself); trying it before "New Orleans" would
   FALSE-PASS the New-Orleans junk rows. Pairs-first prevents that.
5. Verdict = the existing caption-route best/conflict logic over the candidate set (nearest <=50 km
   -> sheet_mention pass; nearest far -> none+conflict; else plain none). Title-preference for
   PASSES was tested and rejected: the cenabum New-Orleans junk names the place only in the DESC and
   box 2 demands the conflict there.
6. STOP list mined from the 354 rows' capitalized-word frequency table (EN metadata labels + NL/FR
   field words + adjectives English/Roman/...); NEVER stop place-name components (New, Great, Old,
   North/South, City, Church, Tower, River, Park, Garden). v2's list accidentally DROPPED the
   preposition block (The/Of/De/Van/La/Le...) and "ordnance/quartier/house/design/pattern/textile/
   numerisation/onderdeel" - those additions + the digit exclusion are still PENDING (my edit call
   failed atomically); they are listed in the task hand-over.
Expected after the fixes: ~1400-1600 unique candidates (v2 measured 2078); the tail is person names
(authors/artists/publishers of the ~180 book-scan rows) - harmless (no P625, the distance guard is
the mechanism), just one-off search cost (~25-35 min at 1 req/s).
Accepted consequence (logged per the iter-14 rule): a city name shared with a person/family name
("York" on the 1914 Christmas tin on york-castle, "Dudley" on a genealogy book) will pass when the
site sits in that city - keyword+50km cannot distinguish a depiction from a name mention; the
never-pass-far guarantee holds and box 1 only needs the majority of the genuinely-naming rows (the
40 Clifford rows pass via the part route, which runs before sheet).
Part-route check: EXACTLY 40 of the 354 none rows name a has_part of their own site (all
Clifford's Tower) - matches the iter-15 census.

## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: check_sheet re-runs the extractor instead of a norm-substring check - agent c's "candidates are norm-substrings by construction" claim is false
Measured: 392 of the 1483 sheet candidates fail `norm(candidate) in norm(title+desc)` (e.g. "Robert Dennis" out of "Robert N. Dennis" - a 1-letter non-kept token survives in norm(hay) and splits the pair; "York Castle" out of "New York. Castle Garden"). check_sheet therefore requires the stored named_place to be in `sheet_candidates(title, desc)` (the extractor is deterministic and offline - the same re-derive-from-printed-text pattern check_book uses for the matcher). check_part keeps norm-substring (a part name is the catalog's own string, norm applied symmetrically both sides - always re-verifies). Pitfall filed separately.

## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: an edit dropped the `def check_none(ev):` line (NameError at check runtime) - caught by the real-file check run before committing, restored in the same session
When inserting check_part/check_sheet after check_caption, the edit's oldText ended at `def check_none(ev):` but the newText did not repeat it, leaving check_none's body as orphaned dead code under check_sheet and a NameError in check_commons. Symptom: `fit/evidence.py check` dies with `NameError: name 'check_none' is not defined`. Lesson: when a block insertion lands between two functions, the trailing `def` line of the NEXT function belongs in the newText.

## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: sheet verdict is a FIRST-RESOLVED WALK, not "nearest <=50 km wins" (iter-16's two statements were contradictory)
Iter 17 (agent c). Iter-16 said both "nearest <=50 km -> pass; nearest far -> conflict" and "pairs-before-
singles prevents the New-Orleans false pass". Both true only if the walk is order-dependent: iterate the
ordered candidates (per run: pairs, then 3-word phrases, then singles; title before desc) and let the
FIRST candidate that resolves decide the row (<=50 km pass / >50 km conflict); unresolved candidates are
skipped. Verified the "nearest wins" variant is WRONG on three real rows: "York" (a word inside "New
York") resolves ~2 km from york-castle and would pass the 4 NYC "Castle Garden" rows; "Orleans" (inside
"New Orleans") resolves onto cenabum itself and would pass the 13 New-Orleans rows; "York Castle" (glued
from "New York. Castle Garden") resolves to the site itself and would pass the same 4 rows. Routes cascade
caption -> part -> sheet; the first route returning pass or conflict decides (a caption conflict preempts
sheet, e.g. the 2 "detail Limmel" rows whose caption names "Bethlehem" -> Q5776 ~4700 km; a 3rd Limmel row
without that word still conflicts, because Maastricht is ~290 km from bethlehem-q2523914's stored point -
6.61E 53.38N is NOT near Maastricht).

## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: castrum-levefanum is the DUTCH Limes fort, not Beauvais - the "Peutinger Casaromago" rows conflict on BOTH limes sites (census correction)
Iter 17 (agent c). The iter-14 census expected the "Peutinger Casaromago.jpg" row to PASS on
castrum-levefanum "~Beauvais". Wrong: the catalog site (Q1309559) is stored at (5.353E, 51.963N) - the
Dutch Lower German Limes identification (region netherlands, part_of Q1265889), 314-368 km from
Beauvais/Amiens. Live-checked this session (3 small wbsearchentities/wbgetentities queries): "Casaromago"
and "Sammarobriva" have NO Wikidata items (search returns []), and "Peutinger" first-P625 is Q2081791
(Peutinger-Gymnasium Ellwangen, 10.13E 48.97N) = 475-500 km from both Dutch sites. So the first-resolved
walk lands on "Peutinger" (far) -> the row records kind none WITH a conflict on BOTH fletio and
castrum-levefanum (iter-14's "passes on castrum-levefanum ~Beauvais" is void; fletio's "conflict-none"
holding). Net effect: ~1-2 fewer genuinely-naming rows pass than the iter-14 census said (~83-88 of
~85-90 instead of the full set) - the box-1 majority still holds. Lesson: never assume a site's geography
from its name; the catalog stores the Dutch identification. (PITFALLS entries for this fact and the
Latin-name/no-item fact are PENDING - the context limit cut the session before PITFALLS.md could be
edited; add them next session.)

Castle, Monmouthshire), dudley-castle 2 (Dudley, Birmingham), plus Darlington (barnard,

(Moll's Somerset Shire map), Alfred Jewel (Q762738, the jewel found at Athelney; 49.25

tests/golden/evidence_check.txt byte-identical (it contains none of the 354). Whole-file

74 caption), 0 resolving <=50 km.

Shortfall vs the iter-17/18 expectation (~10-15 genuinely-naming rows that stayed
none), all fail-closed, in three classes: (1) FIRST-HIT DISAMBIGUATION FAILURES in the
walk - "Cranbourne" -> Q3309297 (Victoria, AUSTRALIA) on the Hampshire OSD sheet (the
UK village has no item; the search's first hit with P625 is abroad), "City" -> Q90

(4.8422E) sits 59 km EAST of Reims city centre (Q41876 at 4.0286E), so the 2
Reims-titled map rows plus 3 junk-first rows conflict at >50 km from the same city's

not this one's files; (3) junk candidates in book scans resolving first (France, Rhin,
Ecole, Ville, Vandernoot on 6 argentoratum rows; artist names like Thomas/Claude/Rosa on
most of the 32 barnard-castle rows). A disambiguation upgrade (multi-hit resolution or
region-biased searching) is the natural follow-up to recover these; not done here - the
50 km guard is this task's mechanism and it held.

New name-sharing classes accepted (logged per the hand-over's step 2c): (a) a
town/county/district containing the site named (Somerset, Monmouthshire, Dudley,
Birmingham, Pontefract, Darlington, Gorton, Nijmegen, Straatsburg, Neudorf) - the file
genuinely depicts that place and the site is inside it; (b) a landmark found at or in
